The key statements are Propositions 3.14 and 3.16 below. The constructions rely on an analysis of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{(2)}$$\end{document}P(2), which in turn is facilitated by the approximations (2.6) and (2.7) and a study of the corresponding linearized Koopman operator, defined for continuous real valued functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi $$\end{document}ϕ on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}$$\end{document}T×R+ by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TP \phi (x,u) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi (T_\omega (x), DT_\omega (x)u)\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPϕ(x,u)=Eϕ(Tω(x),DTω(x)u).Formulas for the mentioned sub- and supermartingales are obtained from a study of the twisted Koopman operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${P}_q$$\end{document}Pq whose action on a real valued function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi $$\end{document}ψ on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {T}$$\end{document}T is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {P}_q \psi (x) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac{\psi \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Lambda (q) = \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n}\ln \left( \mathbb {E} \left[ D T_\omega ^n (x)^q \right] \right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Λ(q)=limn→∞1nlnEDTωn(x)q.The moment Lyapunov exponent plays a central role in our analysis, similar to [11, 13]. Originally introduced by Molchanov [55] and subsequently developed in particular by Arnold [3], it can be viewed as a generalization of the Lyapunov exponent [25]. The relationship between the moment Lyapunov exponent and the Lyapunov exponent was first described in [26].

The above comments on methodology refer to a study of the two-point motion near the diagonal. Due to the non-injectivity of T, trajectories of distinct points \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2 {\setminus } \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2\Δ may land on the diagonal \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ in finite time. Our analysis circumvents this issue in two ways. First, the local estimates for the Koopman operators are restricted to strips \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _{R} \setminus \Delta $$\end{document}ΔR\Δ where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R < R_{min}$$\end{document}R<Rmin. Within this radius, the map acts injectively, ensuring that distinct points no further than R apart do not coalesce in a single iterate, allowing for well-defined expansion and contraction estimates. Second, regarding the global dynamics and measures construction in Sect. 5, the set of points that map directly onto \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ (and thus leave \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {T}^2 \setminus \Delta $$\end{document}T2\Δ) is controlled by Hypothesis (H4). This ensures that the probability of landing on or close to the diagonal from a large distance is negligible for our growth rate estimates.

Koopman Operators

This section develops theory of Koopman operators needed for our analysis on random dynamics. The contents of this section are crucial for the arguments in the following sections, although the statements in the lemmas and propositions in the following sections can be read without reference to this section.

Below we define in particular the annealed Koopman operator and the linearized Koopman operator for the random one-point maps, and the annealed Koopman operator for the random two-point maps. What we refer to as the annealed Koopman operator is also known, especially in the stochastics literature, as the Markov semigroup. We consider the twisted Koopman operator and the moment Lyapunov exponent and use it to obtain eigenfunctions for the linearized Koopman operator. The approximation of the two-point random maps applied to nearby points by the linearized random map allows us to obtain, from these eigenfunctions, functions on which the two-point Koopman operator acts in a desired way: these functions appear in the construction of sub- and supermartingales for the random two-point maps considered near the diagonal.

Twisted Koopman operator and moment Lyapunov function

Write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}C(T,R) for the space of real valued continuous functions on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {T}$$\end{document}T. Denote the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal C^0$$\end{document}C0-norm as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert $$\end{document}‖·‖. The (annealed) Koopman operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P: \mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})\rightarrow \mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}P:C(T,R)→C(T,R) is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P \phi (x)&:=\mathbb {E} \left[ \phi (T_\omega (x) ) \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pϕ(x):=Eϕ(Tω(x)).The twisted Koopman operator, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${P}_q: \mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb R) \rightarrow \mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb R)$$\end{document}Pq:C(T,R)→C(T,R) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q \in \mathbb R$$\end{document}q∈R, is defined for continuous functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi : \mathbb {T}\rightarrow \mathbb R$$\end{document}ψ:T→R by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {P}_q \psi (x) :=\mathbb {E} \left[ \frac{\psi \left( T_\omega (x)\right) }{(D T_\omega (x))^q}\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pqψ(x):=EψTω(x)(DTω(x))q.Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P = {P}_0$$\end{document}P=P0.

The following lemma establishes basic properties of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq. Similar statements in other settings are in [3, 39].

Lemma 3.1

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}q∈R, the operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}C(T,R) is strictly positive and compact.

Proof

It is clear that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q \psi \ge 0$$\end{document}Pqψ≥0 if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \ge 0$$\end{document}ψ≥0, that is, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq is positive. Hypothesis (H2) yields the following property. There exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n∈N, so that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \in \mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}ψ∈C(T,R) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \ge 0$$\end{document}ψ≥0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \ne 0$$\end{document}ψ≠0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q^n \psi > 0$$\end{document}Pqnψ>0 everywhere.

This means that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq is strictly positive.

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}$$\end{document}B be the unit ball in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {C}(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}C(T,R). For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \in \mathcal {B}$$\end{document}ψ∈B, note that (for readability skipping the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pmod 1$$\end{document}(mod1) from the arguments)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P_q \psi (x) = \frac{1}{2\vartheta } \int _{-\vartheta }^\vartheta D T(x+\omega )^{-q} \psi (T(x+\omega ))\, d\omega = \frac{1}{2\vartheta } \int _{x-\vartheta }^{x+\vartheta } DT (y)^{-q} \psi (T(y))\, dy \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pqψ(x)=12ϑ∫-ϑϑDT(x+ω)-qψ(T(x+ω))dω=12ϑ∫x-ϑx+ϑDT(y)-qψ(T(y))dyis a continuously differentiable function of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}x∈T. As \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert P_q \psi \Vert \le C$$\end{document}‖Pqψ‖≤C for a constant C (for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q>0$$\end{document}q>0 we can take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C = a_2^{q}/\vartheta $$\end{document}C=a2q/ϑ, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q<0$$\end{document}q<0 we can take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C = a_1^{q}/\vartheta $$\end{document}C=a1q/ϑ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_1,a_2$$\end{document}a1,a2 from Hypothesis (H1)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq is a bounded operator.

Now\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| D P_q \psi (x) \right|&= \frac{1}{2\vartheta } \left| DT (x+\vartheta )^{-q} \psi (T(x+\vartheta )) - DT (x-\vartheta )^{-q} \psi (T(x-\vartheta )) \right| \\&\le C \Vert \psi \Vert \end{aligned}$$\end{document}DPqψ(x)=12ϑDT(x+ϑ)-qψ(T(x+ϑ))-DT(x-ϑ)-qψ(T(x-ϑ))≤C‖ψ‖for the same constant C. It follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q \psi $$\end{document}Pqψ for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \in \mathcal {B}$$\end{document}ψ∈B is an equicontinuous family of functions. By the Arzela–Ascoli theorem we get that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq is a compact operator.

Using this lemma we get the following results on the dominant eigenvalue and corresponding eigenvector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq.

We make use of the qth moment Lyapunov exponent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q)$$\end{document}Λ(q) (see [3, 12, 39]) defined as3.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Lambda (q) :=\lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n}\ln \left( \mathbb {E} \left[ D T_\omega ^n (x)^q \right] \right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Λ(q):=limn→∞1nlnEDTωn(x)q.We also write moment Lyapunov function, especially when discussing its dependence on q. We will find that the limit does not depend on x, and that it exists as an analytic function of q. Denote \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\!\!1_\mathbb {T}:\mathbb {T}\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}1T:T→R as the constant function equal to 1 on the circle.

Proposition 3.2

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}q∈R, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${P}_{q}$$\end{document}Pq has a dominant simple eigenvalue \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{\Lambda (-q)}$$\end{document}eΛ(-q). The rest of the spectrum of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq is contained in a disk of radius less then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{\Lambda (-q)}$$\end{document}eΛ(-q). Write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _q \in \mathcal {C} (\mathbb {T},\mathbb R)$$\end{document}ϕq∈C(T,R) for the dominant eigenfunction of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${P}_{q}$$\end{document}Pq, so3.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {P}_{q}\phi _{q} = e^{\Lambda (-q)} \phi _{q}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pqϕq=eΛ(-q)ϕq.Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _0 = 1\!\!1_\mathbb {T}$$\end{document}ϕ0=1T and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{\Lambda (0)} = 1$$\end{document}eΛ(0)=1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _q$$\end{document}ϕq is a positive function,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q)$$\end{document}Λ(q) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _{q}$$\end{document}ϕq depend analytically on q,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ is convex,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q) \ge \lambda q$$\end{document}Λ(q)≥λq.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _0 = 1\!\!1_\mathbb {T}$$\end{document}ϕ0=1T and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{\Lambda (0)} = 1$$\end{document}eΛ(0)=1,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _0 = 1\!\!1_\mathbb {T}$$\end{document}ϕ0=1T and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{\Lambda (0)} = 1$$\end{document}eΛ(0)=1,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _q$$\end{document}ϕq is a positive function,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _q$$\end{document}ϕq is a positive function,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q)$$\end{document}Λ(q) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _{q}$$\end{document}ϕq depend analytically on q,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q)$$\end{document}Λ(q) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _{q}$$\end{document}ϕq depend analytically on q,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ is convex,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ is convex,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q) \ge \lambda q$$\end{document}Λ(q)≥λq.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q) \ge \lambda q$$\end{document}Λ(q)≥λq.

Proof

The proof follows ideas as in [3, 39]. The spectral properties of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq follow from Lemma 3.1 and the Krein-Rutman theorem (see for instance [30, Section 19.5]). Write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r(q) = \sigma \left( P_{q} \right) $$\end{document}r(q)=σPq for the spectral radius of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq. By the Krein-Rutman theorem, this equals the dominant eigenvalue of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q$$\end{document}Pq. The dominant eigenvalue is simple, because the operator is strictly positive. We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P^n_q1\!\!1_\mathbb {T} (x) = \langle k_q, 1\!\!1_\mathbb {T} \rangle r(q)^n \phi _q (x) + o(r(q)^n), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pqn1T(x)=⟨kq,1T⟩r(q)nϕq(x)+o(r(q)n),as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n→∞, uniformly in x, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_q\in \mathcal C(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})^* $$\end{document}kq∈C(T,R)∗ is a probability measure, see [3]. Now we rescale \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _q$$\end{document}ϕq, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle k_q, 1\!\!1_\mathbb {T} \rangle =1$$\end{document}⟨kq,1T⟩=1 and using this, calculate\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n} \ln \left( \mathbb {E} \left[ \left( DT_\omega ^n (x) \right) ^{-q} \right] \right)&= \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\frac{1}{n} \ln \left( P^n_q1\!\!1_\mathbb {T} (x) \right) \\&= \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\ln \left( \left( P^n_q1\!\!1_\mathbb {T} (x) \right) ^{1/n} \right) \\&= \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \ln \left( \left( r(q)^n \phi _q (x) + o(r(q)^n) \right) ^{1/n} \right) \\&= \ln (r(q)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞1nlnEDTωn(x)-q=limn→∞1nlnPqn1T(x)=limn→∞lnPqn1T(x)1/n=limn→∞lnr(q)nϕq(x)+o(r(q)n)1/n=ln(r(q)).We find that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n} \ln \left( \mathbb {E} \left[ \left( DT_\omega ^n (x) \right) ^{-q} \right] \right) $$\end{document}limn→∞1nlnEDTωn(x)-q does not depend on x. The remaining properties of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (-q)$$\end{document}Λ(-q) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _q$$\end{document}ϕq follow from [3, 39].

The following lemma connects the moment Lyapunov exponent and the Lyapunov exponent.

Lemma 3.3

The first derivative of the moment Lyapunov function at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q=0$$\end{document}q=0 is equal to the Lyapunov exponent3.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Lambda ' (0)&= \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n} \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln \left( D T_\omega ^n (x) \right) \right] = \lambda . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Λ′(0)=limn→∞1nElnDTωn(x)=λ.

Proof

This follows from the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (0) =0$$\end{document}Λ(0)=0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q) \ge \lambda q$$\end{document}Λ(q)≥λq and the analyticity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ. For the complete argument, see [3, 39].

We have established that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ is a convex function that vanishes at 0. The following lemma shows the existence of a second zero if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda \ne 0$$\end{document}λ≠0. This second zero plays a prominent role in our analysis and also appears in the statements of the main theorem on stationary measures.

Lemma 3.4

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda \ne 0$$\end{document}λ≠0 then there is a unique \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \ne 0$$\end{document}γ≠0, with opposite sign, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (\gamma ) = 0$$\end{document}Λ(γ)=0. (We set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma = 0$$\end{document}γ=0 if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda = 0$$\end{document}λ=0.)

Proof

Assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda > 0$$\end{document}λ>0, which implies by (3.3) that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda '(0) > 0$$\end{document}Λ′(0)>0. By Hypothesis (H3) there is a contracting periodic point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_c$$\end{document}xc of T. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_c$$\end{document}kc denote its period. By continuity of the map, there exist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon > 0$$\end{document}ε>0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta > 0$$\end{document}δ>0 such that the set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {H}^c = \big \{ \omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta \;; \; x \in B_\varepsilon (x_c) \text { implies } T^{k_c}_\omega (x) \in B_\varepsilon (x_c),\, \ln (DT_\omega ^{k_c}(x)) < -\delta \big \} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hc={ω∈Σϑ;x∈Bε(xc)impliesTωc(x)∈Bε(xc),ln(DTωc(x))<-δ}has positive measure \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {P} ( \mathcal {H}^c ) > 0$$\end{document}P(Hc)>0.

By Hypothesis (H2), we know that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}x∈T, there is a positive probability of reaching \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B_\varepsilon (x_c)$$\end{document}Bε(xc) in k steps. By continuity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q)$$\end{document}Λ(q) and the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda '(0) > 0$$\end{document}Λ′(0)>0, there is a unique \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma < 0$$\end{document}γ<0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (\gamma ) = 0$$\end{document}Λ(γ)=0.

The argument in the case where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda < 0$$\end{document}λ<0 is analogous, using an expanding periodic orbit instead of the contracting periodic orbit. Lemma 3.5

Proof

It is immediate from the convexity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V \ge 0$$\end{document}V≥0. Lemma 3.6

We have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V>0$$\end{document}V>0.

Proof

Following [12] we establish that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V = 0$$\end{document}V=0 implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (q) = \lambda q$$\end{document}Λ(q)=λq, which will lead to a contradiction.

Rewriting (3.8), we obtain3.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & V + \left( P_0 - I\right) \left( (\partial _q \phi _0 )^2 - \partial ^2_q \phi _0 \right) (x) \nonumber \\ & \quad = \mathbb {E}\left[ \left( \partial _q \phi _0 (T_\omega (x)) - \ln (DT_\omega (x)) \right) ^2 \right] - \left( \partial _q \phi _0 (x) - \lambda \right) ^2 \nonumber \\ & \quad = \mathbb {E}\left[ \left( \partial _q \phi _0 (T_\omega (x)) - \ln (DT_\omega (x)) \right) ^2 \right] - \left( \mathbb {E}\left[ \partial _q \phi _0 (T_\omega (x)) - \ln (DT_\omega (x)) \right] \right) ^2.\nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}V+P0-I(∂qϕ0)2-∂q2ϕ0(x)=E∂qϕ0(Tω(x))-ln(DTω(x))2-∂qϕ0(x)-λ2=E∂qϕ0(Tω(x))-ln(DTω(x))2-E∂qϕ0(Tω(x))-ln(DTω(x))2.The last step uses (3.4). We conclude that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V + \left( P_0 - I\right) \left( (\partial _q \phi _0 )^2 - \partial ^2_q \phi _0 \right) (x) \ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}V+P0-I(∂qϕ0)2-∂q2ϕ0(x)≥0.Now suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V=0$$\end{document}V=0, for the sake of contradiction. From \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( P_0 - I\right) \left( (\partial _q \phi _0 )^2 - \partial ^2_q \phi _0 \right) (x) \ge 0$$\end{document}P0-I(∂qϕ0)2-∂q2ϕ0(x)≥0 we conclude that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\partial _q \phi _0 )^2 - \partial ^2_q \phi _0$$\end{document}(∂qϕ0)2-∂q2ϕ0 is constant (to see this, note that if a continuous function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\chi :\mathbb {T} \rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}χ:T→R takes a maximum at x, then the property \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E} \left[ \chi (T_\omega (x)) \right] \ge \chi (x)$$\end{document}Eχ(Tω(x))≥χ(x) with Hypothesis (H2) implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\chi $$\end{document}χ is maximal at every point of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {T}$$\end{document}T). So in fact\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left( P_0 - I\right) \left( (\partial _q \phi _0 )^2 - \partial ^2_q \phi _0 \right) (x) = 0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P0-I(∂qϕ0)2-∂q2ϕ0(x)=0and (3.9) implies that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _q \phi _0 (T_\omega (x)) - \ln (DT_\omega (x)) = \partial _q \phi _0 (x) - \lambda \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂qϕ0(Tω(x))-ln(DTω(x))=∂qϕ0(x)-λfor all x and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω. Lemma 3.4 shows that this is not the case in our set-up. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Linearized Koopman operator

We introduce the linearized Koopman operator, defined for continuous real valued functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi $$\end{document}ϕ on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}$$\end{document}T×R+ by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TP \phi (x,u) :=\mathbb {E} \left[ \phi (T_\omega (x), DT_\omega (x)u)\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPϕ(x,u):=Eϕ(Tω(x),DTω(x)u).Recall from Proposition 3.2 that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _q$$\end{document}ϕq is the dominant eigenfunction of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${P}_q$$\end{document}Pq. Define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{W}_q:{\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}W~q:T×R+→R by3.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{W}_q(x,u)&= u^{-q}\phi _q\left( x\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}W~q(x,u)=u-qϕqx.We reserve the tilde notation for functions on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+$$\end{document}T×R+.

Lemma 3.7

The function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{W}_q$$\end{document}W~q is an eigenfunction of TP, with eigenvalue \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${e^{\Lambda (-q)}}$$\end{document}eΛ(-q):3.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TP \tilde{W}_q (x,u) = e^{\Lambda (-q)} \tilde{W}_q (x,u). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPW~q(x,u)=eΛ(-q)W~q(x,u).

Proof

This follows from a straightforward computation. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,u) \in {\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}$$\end{document}(x,u)∈T×R+,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TP \tilde{W}_q (x,u)&= \mathbb {E} \left[ \tilde{W}_q(T_\omega (x),D T_\omega (x) u)\right] \\&= \mathbb {E} \left[ (DT_\omega (x) u)^{-q}\phi _q\left( T_\omega (x)\right) \right] \\&= u^{-q}\mathbb {E} \left[ DT_\omega (x) ^{-q}\phi _q\left( T_\omega (x)\right) \right] \\&= u^{-q} {P}_q\phi _q(x)\\&= e^{\Lambda (-q)} u^{-q} \phi _q(x)\\&= e^{\Lambda (-q)}\tilde{W}_q(x,u). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPW~q(x,u)=EW~q(Tω(x),DTω(x)u)=E(DTω(x)u)-qϕqTω(x)=u-qEDTω(x)-qϕqTω(x)=u-qPqϕq(x)=eΛ(-q)u-qϕq(x)=eΛ(-q)W~q(x,u).

Remark 3.8

Consider a random sequence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (x_{n+1}, u_{n+1}) = \left( T_{\sigma ^n\omega } (x_n), DT_{\sigma ^n\omega } (x_n) u_n\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(xn+1,un+1)=Tσnω(xn),DTσnω(xn)un,with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_0,u_0 \in \mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+$$\end{document}x0,u0∈T×R+. Take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma $$\end{document}γ as in Lemma 3.4 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{\Lambda (\gamma )}=1$$\end{document}eΛ(γ)=1. Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (TP - I) \tilde{W}_\gamma (x,u) = 0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(TP-I)W~γ(x,u)=0,which shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{W}_\gamma (x_n,u_n)$$\end{document}W~γ(xn,un) is a martingale. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proof of Lemma 3.10

We give a constructive proof for Lemma 3.10, where we use Proposition 3.2 and Lemma 3.5. We first find the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\phi }$$\end{document}ϕ~ for which (3.12) and (3.13) holds. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ (x,u) \in {\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}$$\end{document}(x,u)∈T×R+, let3.16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{\phi }(x,u)&= \ln (u) -{\partial _q \phi _0} (x). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕ~(x,u)=ln(u)-∂qϕ0(x).Note that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{\phi }\in \mathcal {C}^0 ({\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+},\mathbb R). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕ~∈C0(T×R+,R).Furthermore, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,u) \in {\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}$$\end{document}(x,u)∈T×R+, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left( TP-I\right) \tilde{\phi } (x,u)&= \left( TP-I\right) \ln (\pi _2)(x,u) -\left( P_0-I\right) \left( {\partial _q \phi _0} \right) (x) \\&= \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln ( DT_\omega (x) u )\right] - For the second equality we apply (3.4) in Lemma 3.5.

For the second part of the proof, we take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda =0$$\end{document}λ=0 and let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{\eta }(x,u) = \ln ^2(u) -2\ln (u){\partial _q \phi _0} (x) +{\partial ^2_q \phi _0} (x). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}η~(x,u)=ln2(u)-2ln(u)∂qϕ0(x)+∂q2ϕ0(x).And again note that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{\eta }\in \mathcal {C}^0 ({\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+},\mathbb R). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}η~∈C0(T×R+,R).From a straightforward computation, we obtain, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,u)\in {\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}$$\end{document}(x,u)∈T×R+\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \left( TP-I\right) \eta (x,u) = \left( TP-I\right) \ln ^2(\pi _2)(x,u)\\ & \quad - Remark 3.11

Consider a random sequence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (x_{n+1}, u_{n+1}) = \left( T_{\sigma ^n\omega } (x_n), DT_{\sigma ^n\omega } (x_n) u_n\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(xn+1,un+1)=Tσnω(xn),DTσnω(xn)un,with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_0,u_0) \in \mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+$$\end{document}(x0,u0)∈T×R+. Assuming \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda =0$$\end{document}λ=0, (3.15) expresses that the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\eta }(x_n,u_n) - nV$$\end{document}η~(xn,un)-nV is a martingale. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Example 3.12

In the case of Example 1.3, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\vartheta = 0.5$$\end{document}ϑ=0.5, we get \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\phi } (x,u) = \ln (u) + \Vert \partial _q\phi _0\Vert $$\end{document}ϕ~(x,u)=ln(u)+‖∂qϕ0‖ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\eta }(x,u) = \ln ^2(u)-2\Vert \partial _q\phi _0\Vert \ln (u) + \Vert \partial _q^2\phi _0\Vert $$\end{document}η~(x,u)=ln2(u)-2‖∂qϕ0‖ln(u)+‖∂q2ϕ0‖. Both functions are independent of x, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_q\psi $$\end{document}Pqψ is constant for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \in \mathcal C(\mathbb {T},\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}ψ∈C(T,R). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 3.13

There exist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R,B>0$$\end{document}R,B>0, such that we have the following bounds, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y)\in \Delta _R\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ,3.24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| TP\tilde{\phi }(x,d(x,y)) - P_{(2)}\phi (x,y)) \right|&\le B d(x,y), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPϕ~(x,d(x,y))-P(2)ϕ(x,y))≤Bd(x,y),3.25\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| TP\tilde{\eta }(x,d(x,y)) - P_{(2)} \eta (x,y)\right|&\le B d(x,y)|\ln (d(x,y))|, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPη~(x,d(x,y))-P(2)η(x,y)≤Bd(x,y)|ln(d(x,y))|,and for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q \in [-|\gamma | -1, |\gamma | +1 ]$$\end{document}q∈[-|γ|-1,|γ|+1],3.26\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| TP\tilde{W}_q(x,d(x,y))-P_{(2)}W_q(x,y)\right| \le B d(x,y)^{-q+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPW~q(x,d(x,y))-P(2)Wq(x,y)≤Bd(x,y)-q+1.

Proof

We will work out the estimates for (3.24), then the computation for (3.25) is analogous. We will sketch the proof for (3.26).

Recall that w(x, y) denotes the signed distance between x and y, for nearby points \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x, y \in \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}x,y∈T. Take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R >0$$\end{document}R>0 small enough so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_\omega $$\end{document}Tω is injective on all intervals of length R. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{R}\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ,3.27\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TP \tilde{\phi }(x,|w(x,y)|)&= \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln (|DT_\omega (x) w(x,y)| )\right] - P_0{\partial _q \phi _0} (x) \nonumber \\&= \ln (|w(x,y)|) + \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln (DT_\omega (x)) \right] -\mathbb {E} \left[ {\partial _q \phi _0} \left( T_\omega (x)\right) \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPϕ~(x,|w(x,y)|)=Eln(|DTω(x)w(x,y)|)-P0∂qϕ0(x)=ln(|w(x,y)|)+Eln(DTω(x))-E∂qϕ0Tω(x).To determine \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{(2)}\phi $$\end{document}P(2)ϕ, we use a Taylor expansion for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_\omega $$\end{document}Tω. We obtain,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w(T_\omega ^{(2)}(x,y)) = DT_\omega (x)w(x,y) +\frac{1}{2} D^2 T_\omega (\xi )w(x,y)^2 , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}w(Tω(2)(x,y))=DTω(x)w(x,y)+12D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)2,for a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi $$\end{document}ξ in the interval between x and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x + w(x,y)$$\end{document}x+w(x,y). Therefore, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{R}\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R>0$$\end{document}R>0 small enough,3.28\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & P_{(2)} \phi (x,y) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln \left( \left| w(T_\omega ^{(2)}(x,y))\right| \right) \right] -P_0{\partial _q \phi _0} (x) \nonumber \\ & \quad = \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln \left( \left| DT_\omega (x) w(x,y) + \frac{D^2T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)^2}{2} \right| \right) \right] - P_0{\partial _q \phi _0} (x) \nonumber \\ & \quad = \ln (|w(x,y)|) + \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln \left( DT_\omega (x)\right) \right] + \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln \left( \left| 1 +\frac{D^2T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)}{2 DT_\omega (x)} \right| \right) \right] \nonumber \\ & \qquad - \mathbb {E} [{\partial _q \phi _0} (T_\omega (x))]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(2)ϕ(x,y)=Elnw(Tω(2)(x,y))-P0∂qϕ0(x)=ElnDTω(x)w(x,y)+D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)22-P0∂qϕ0(x)=ln(|w(x,y)|)+ElnDTω(x)+Eln1+D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)2DTω(x)-E[∂qϕ0(Tω(x))].Combining (3.27) and (3.28) yields, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{R}\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ,3.29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| P_{(2)} \phi (x,y) - For R small we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left| \frac{D^2 T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)}{2DT_\omega (x)}\right| < 1$$\end{document}D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)2DTω(x)<1 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{R}{\setminus } \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ. Then for the upper bound we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} To prove (3.25), we again perform an explicit calculation. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta = \ln ^2(d(x,y)) - 2 \partial _q \phi _0 (x)\ln (d(x,y)) + \partial _q\phi _0(x)$$\end{document}η=ln2(d(x,y))-2∂qϕ0(x)ln(d(x,y))+∂qϕ0(x) is made up from three terms. When bounding the difference \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left| TP\tilde{\eta }(x,d(x,y)) - P_{(2)} \eta (x,y)\right| $$\end{document}TPη~(x,d(x,y))-P(2)η(x,y), the contribution from the last two terms is treated as above.

It therefore suffices to bound the contribution from the first term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ln ^2(d(x,y))$$\end{document}ln2(d(x,y)).

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{R}\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ, factoring the Taylor expansion as in the previous step, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P_{(2)} \ln ^2(d) (x,y)&= \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln ^2\left( \left| DT_\omega (x)w(x,y) + \frac{D^2T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)^2}{2} \right| \right) \right] \\&= \mathbb {E} \left[ \left( \ln (|DT_\omega (x)w(x,y)|) + \ln \left( \left| 1 +\frac{D^2T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)}{2 DT_\omega (x)} \right| \right) \right) ^2 \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(2)ln2(d)(x,y)=Eln2DTω(x)w(x,y)+D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)22=Eln(|DTω(x)w(x,y)|)+ln1+D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)2DTω(x)2.Expanding the square and comparing with the term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E}[\ln ^2(|DT_\omega (x)w(x,y)|)]$$\end{document}E[ln2(|DTω(x)w(x,y)|)] from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TP \tilde{\eta }$$\end{document}TPη~, we find that the difference is bounded by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\left| P_{(2)} \eta (x,y) - TP \tilde{\eta } (x,d(x,y)) \right| \\&\quad \le \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| 2\ln (|DT_\omega (x)w(x,y)|) \ln \left( 1 +\frac{D^2T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)}{2 DT_\omega (x)} \right) \right| \right] \\&\qquad + \mathbb {E} \left[ \ln ^2\left( 1 +\frac{D^2T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)}{2 DT_\omega (x)} \right) \right] + O(d(x,y)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(2)η(x,y)-TPη~(x,d(x,y))≤E2ln(|DTω(x)w(x,y)|)ln1+D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)2DTω(x)+Eln21+D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)2DTω(x)+O(d(x,y)).Using the inequality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^2/(1+u)^2\le \ln ^2(1+u) \le u^2$$\end{document}u2/(1+u)2≤ln2(1+u)≤u2 for small u (as in (3.29)), the second term is bounded by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(d(x,y)^2)$$\end{document}O(d(x,y)2). Thus,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| P_{(2)} \eta (x,y) - TP \tilde{\eta } (x,d(x,y)) \right|&\le 2 C |\ln (d(x,y))| \cdot \frac{|w(x,y)| \Vert D^2T_\omega \Vert }{2 a_1} + O(d(x,y)) \\&\le B d(x,y) |\ln (d(x,y))|. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(2)η(x,y)-TPη~(x,d(x,y))≤2C|ln(d(x,y))|·|w(x,y)|‖D2Tω‖2a1+O(d(x,y))≤Bd(x,y)|ln(d(x,y))|.We can choose B such that (3.25) holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{R}\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ with R small enough.
