Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \left| TP\tilde{W_q}(x,d(x,y))-P_{(2)}W_q(x,y)\right| \nonumber \\ & \quad \le \left\| \phi _q\right\| \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \left| DT_\omega (x) w(x,y) \right| ^{-q} - \left| DT_\omega (x) w(x,y) +\frac{1}{2} D^2T_\omega (\xi ) w(x,y)^2 \right| ^{-q} \right| \right] . \nonumber \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPWq~(x,d(x,y))-P(2)Wq(x,y)≤ϕqEDTω(x)w(x,y)-q-DTω(x)w(x,y)+12D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)2-q.With () and the mean value theorem for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a \mapsto a^{-q}$$\end{document}a↦a-q, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| TP\tilde{W_q}(x,w(x,y))-P_{(2)}W_q(x,y)\right|&\le C_q |q| \mathbb {E} \left[ |w(x,y)|^{-q-1}|D^2T_\omega (\xi )w(x,y)|^2 \right] \\&\le C_q |q| \Vert D^2T \Vert ^2 |w(x,y)|^{-q+1} \\&\le B d(x,y)^{-q+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TPWq~(x,w(x,y))-P(2)Wq(x,y)≤Cq|q|E|w(x,y)|-q-1|D2Tω(ξ)w(x,y)|2≤Cq|q|‖D2T‖2|w(x,y)|-q+1≤Bd(x,y)-q+1,for some positive constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_q$$\end{document}Cq. The following lemma is a key lemma that adapts the equalities and estimates in Lemma 3.13 for TP to the setting for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{(2)}$$\end{document}P(2), but only near the diagonal \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ, using that near \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{(2)}$$\end{document}P(2) can be approximated by TP (Lemma 3.13).

Proposition 3.14

There exists a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R,K>0$$\end{document}R,K>0, and continuous integrable functions\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi ^\pm , \eta ^\pm \in \mathcal {C}^0(\mathbb {T}^2\setminus \Delta , \mathbb {R}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕ±,η±∈C0(T2\Δ,R),such that the following holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _R\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ.

Proof

This basically means that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( P_{(2)}-I\right) \phi $$\end{document}P(2)-Iϕ near the diagonal is close to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ. By Proposition 3.2, we obtain3.36\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left( TP-I\right) \tilde{W}_{-q_0}(x,u) = \left( e^{\Lambda (q_0)}-1\right) u^{q_0}\phi _{-q_0}(x), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TP-IW~-q0(x,u)=eΛ(q0)-1uq0ϕ-q0(x),for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,u) \in {\mathbb {T}\times \mathbb {R}^+}$$\end{document}(x,u)∈T×R+.

The second inequality regarding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^-$$\end{document}ϕ- is obtained using similar bounds. The bound (3.30) for suitable \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K>0$$\end{document}K>0 is immediate from the expressions for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^\pm $$\end{document}ϕ±.

Proposition 3.16

So3.42\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| \left( P_{(2)}-e^{\Lambda (-q)}\right) W_q (x,y)\right| \le B d(x,y)^{-q+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(2)-eΛ(-q)Wq(x,y)≤Bd(x,y)-q+1,for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{R}\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR\Δ.

Remark 3.17

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma $$\end{document}γ is such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{\Lambda (\gamma )} = 1$$\end{document}eΛ(γ)=1. We can use (3.41) to get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \pm \mathbb E\left[ W_{-\gamma }^\pm (T_\omega (x),T_\omega (y)) \right] \ge \pm W_{-\gamma }^\pm (x,y), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}±EW-γ±(Tω(x),Tω(y))≥±W-γ±(x,y),for d(x, y) small enough. Applying Doob’s stopping time theorem with suitable stopping times we build sub- and supermartingales for such random sequences from the functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^+_\gamma $$\end{document}Wγ+ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^-_\gamma $$\end{document}Wγ-. This is done in the proof of Lemma 4.5.

Topological Random Dynamics

In this section we prove Theorem 1.1 and construct tools to prove Theorem 1.2 in Sect. 5, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda \ge 0$$\end{document}λ≥0. We look separately at cases with zero Lyapunov exponent, positive Lyapunov exponent and negative Lyapunov exponent.

Zero Lyapunov exponent

The next part of the analysis is to calculate escape probabilities and expected escape times for escape from neighborhoods of the diagonal and strips near the diagonal. The proofs in this section rely on an analysis of Koopman operators, which is developed in Sect. 3. The statements can be read without reference to Sect. 3, but for the proofs the reader has to familiarize with the results in Sect. 3.

In order study escapes from strips near the diagonal, we define stopping times4.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)&:=\min \{n\in \mathbb N \; ; \; d(T_\omega ^n(x), T_\omega ^n(y))> \delta \}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}τδ,+(x,y):=min{n∈N;d(Tωn(x),Tωn(y))>δ},4.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)&:=\min \{n\in \mathbb N \; ; \; d(T_\omega ^n(x), T_\omega ^n(y))< \varepsilon \}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}τε,-(x,y):=min{n∈N;d(Tωn(x),Tωn(y))<ε},where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _R$$\end{document}(x,y)∈ΔR with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<\varepsilon< d(x,y)< \delta <R $$\end{document}0<ε<d(x,y)<δ<R. The following lemma addresses statistics of these stopping times.

Lemma 4.1

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} Proof

We fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R>0$$\end{document}R>0 small enough and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K>0$$\end{document}K>0 large enough such that Proposition 3.14 and Proposition 3.16 hold simultaneously. We follow the reasoning of [11, 13]. As indicated above, we will use statements from Sect. 3. The functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^\pm $$\end{document}ϕ± and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta ^\pm $$\end{document}η± come directly from Proposition 3.14. Denote \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_n,y_n) = T_\omega ^n(x_0,y_0),$$\end{document}(xn,yn)=Tωn(x0,y0), for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_0,y_0)\in \mathbb {T}^2{\setminus }\Delta $$\end{document}(x0,y0)∈T2\Δ, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<\varepsilon<d(x,y)<\delta < R$$\end{document}0<ε<d(x,y)<δ<R. Now \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta ^+(x_n,y_n)$$\end{document}η+(xn,yn) stopped at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\min \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y), \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\}$$\end{document}min{τε,-(x,y),τδ,+(x,y)} is a submartingale, by Proposition 3.14, Remark 3.15 and applying Doob’s stopping time theorem. So, with\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{n} = \min \{n, \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y), \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}n~=min{n,τε,-(x,y),τδ,+(x,y)},we have, for a fixed \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n∈N, by Doob’s stopping time theorem with bounded stopping time,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \eta ^+(x,y) \le \mathbb {E} \left[ \eta ^+(x_{\tilde{n}},y_{\tilde{n}})-\tilde{n}V\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}η+(x,y)≤Eη+(xn~,yn~)-n~V.Rewriting, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E[\tilde{n}] V&\le \mathbb {E} \left[ \eta ^+(x_{\tilde{n}},y_{\tilde{n}})\right] - \eta ^+(x,y)\\&\le \ln ^2(d(x,y))+\ln ^2(\varepsilon a_1) + K|\ln (\varepsilon a_1)|<\infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[n~]V≤Eη+(xn~,yn~)-η+(x,y)≤ln2(d(x,y))+ln2(εa1)+K|ln(εa1)|<∞. different, larger K, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} )+ K|\log (\varepsilon )|$$\end{document}Eη+(xτ,yτ)|τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)≤ln2(ε)+K|log(ε)| and we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} -\eta ^+(x,y)&\le -\ln ^2(d(x,y)) + K \left| \ln (d(x,y)) \right| . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}-η+(x,y)≤-ln2(d(x,y))+Kln(d(x,y)).Plugging this and (4.7), (4.11), (3.32) into the estimate (4.12), we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V\mathbb {E} \left[ \tau \right]\le & \frac{\displaystyle \ln \left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\delta }\right) +4K}{\displaystyle \ln \left( \frac{\varepsilon }{ \delta }\right) }\left( \ln ^2\left( {\varepsilon }\right) -\ln ^2\left( \delta \right) + K \left| \ln \left( \frac{\varepsilon }{\delta } \right) \right| \right) \\ & + \ln ^2(\delta ) + 2K |\ln (\delta )| - \ln ^2(d(x,y)) + 2K |\ln (d(x,y))|. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}VEτ≤lnd(x,y)δ+4Klnεδln2ε-ln2δ+Klnεδ+ln2(δ)+2K|ln(δ)|-ln2(d(x,y))+2K|ln(d(x,y))|.Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ln ^2\left( {\varepsilon }\right) -\ln ^2\left( \delta \right) = \ln \left( \frac{\varepsilon }{\delta } \right) \ln (\varepsilon \delta )$$\end{document}ln2ε-ln2δ=lnεδln(εδ) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ln ^2(\delta ) - \ln ^2(d(x,y)) = \ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)}\right) \ln (d(x,y)\delta )$$\end{document}ln2(δ)-ln2(d(x,y))=lnδd(x,y)ln(d(x,y)δ). As \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\ln (x)|$$\end{document}|ln(x)| is a decreasing function for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<x<1$$\end{document}0<x<1, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\ln (\varepsilon )|< |\ln (d(x,y))| < |\ln (\delta )|$$\end{document}|ln(ε)|<|ln(d(x,y))|<|ln(δ)|. Using these identities and estimates we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V\mathbb {E} \left[ \tau \right]\le & \ln \left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\delta }\right) \ln (\varepsilon \delta ) - \ln \left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\delta }\right) \ln \left( d(x,y)\delta \right) + 12K|\ln (\varepsilon )| + 8K^2 \\\le & \ln \left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\delta }\right) \ln \left( \frac{\varepsilon }{d(x,y)}\right) + 12K|\ln (\varepsilon )| + 8K^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}VEτ≤lnd(x,y)δln(εδ)-lnd(x,y)δlnd(x,y)δ+12K|ln(ε)|+8K2≤lnd(x,y)δlnεd(x,y)+12K|ln(ε)|+8K2.In a similar fashion we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & V\mathbb {E} \left[ \tau \right] \ge \frac{\displaystyle \ln \left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\delta }\right) -4K}{\displaystyle \ln \left( \frac{\varepsilon }{ \delta }\right) }\left( \ln ^2\left( \varepsilon \right) -\ln ^2\left( \delta \right) + 2K \left| \ln \left( \frac{\varepsilon }{\delta } \right) \right| \right) \\ & \quad + \ln ^2(\delta ) - 2K |\ln (\delta )| - \ln ^2(d(x,y)) + 2K |\ln (d(x,y))|, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}VEτ≥lnd(x,y)δ-4Klnεδln2ε-ln2δ+2Klnεδ+ln2(δ)-2K|ln(δ)|-ln2(d(x,y))+2K|ln(d(x,y))|,and from this,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V\mathbb {E} \left[ \tau \right] \ge \ln \left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\delta }\right) \ln \left( \frac{\varepsilon }{d(x,y)}\right) - 12K|\ln (\varepsilon )| - 8K^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}VEτ≥lnd(x,y)δlnεd(x,y)-12K|ln(ε)|-8K2.Replace 2K by K to get the statement of the lemma. This finishes the proof.

From here on, R, K are fixed as in Lemma 4.1. We now formulate a result stating that trajectories of points \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _\delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈Δδ will almost surely escape from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\delta $$\end{document}Δδ. However, the expected escape time will be infinite for d(x, y) sufficiently small.

Lemma 4.2

Let R, K be as in Lemma 4.1. Then for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2$$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<d(x,y)< \delta <R$$\end{document}0<d(x,y)<δ<R,4.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}\left( \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)< \infty \right) = 1 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pτδ,+(x,y)<∞=1and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} \left[ \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right] = \infty \text { whenever } d(x,y) < e^{-6K}\delta . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Eτδ,+(x,y)=∞wheneverd(x,y)<e-6Kδ.

Proof

For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \in (0, d(x,y))$$\end{document}ε∈(0,d(x,y)), we have the inclusion of events\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \{ \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y) \le \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y) \} \subset \{ \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y) < \infty \}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}{τδ,+(x,y)≤τε,-(x,y)}⊂{τδ,+(x,y)<∞}.Therefore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}(\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)< \infty ) \ge \mathbb {P}(\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y) \le \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)) = 1 - \mathbb {P} (\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y) < \tau _{\delta ,+} (x,y)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(τδ,+(x,y)<∞)≥P(τδ,+(x,y)≤τε,-(x,y))=1-P(τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)).Recall the upper bound in (4.4),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P} (\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y) < \tau _{\delta ,+} (x,y)) \le \frac{\ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)}\right) +2K}{\ln \left( \frac{\delta }{\varepsilon }\right) }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y))≤lnδd(x,y)+2Klnδε.As \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}ε→0, the denominator goes to infinity while the numerator remains fixed. Thus the term on the right hand side goes to 0, implying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathbb {P}(\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y) < \infty ) = 1$$\end{document}P(τδ,+(x,y)<∞)=1. For the second statement, observe that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0< \varepsilon < d(x,y)$$\end{document}0<ε<d(x,y),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tau _{\delta ,+} (x,y) \ge \min \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y), \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}τδ,+(x,y)≥min{τε,-(x,y),τδ,+(x,y)}.Taking expectations and applying the lower bound from (4.5) in Lemma 4.1, we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V \mathbb {E}[\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)]&\ge V \mathbb {E}[\min \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y), \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\}] \\ &\ge \ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)} \right) \ln \left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\varepsilon } \right) - 6K | \ln (\varepsilon )| - 2K^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}VE[τδ,+(x,y)]≥VE[min{τε,-(x,y),τδ,+(x,y)}]≥lnδd(x,y)lnd(x,y)ε-6K|ln(ε)|-2K2.Recalling that for small \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon $$\end{document}ε, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \ln (d(x,y)/\varepsilon ) = \ln (d(x,y)) + | \ln (\varepsilon )|$$\end{document}ln(d(x,y)/ε)=ln(d(x,y))+|ln(ε)|, we group the terms involving \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\ln (\varepsilon )|$$\end{document}|ln(ε)|:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V \mathbb {E}[\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)]&\ge \ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)} \right) \left( \ln (d(x,y)) + |\ln (\varepsilon )| \right) - 6K |\ln (\varepsilon )| - 2K^2 \\ &= |\ln (\varepsilon )| \left[ \ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)} \right) - 6K \right] + \ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)} \right) \ln (d(x,y)) - 2K^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}VE[τδ,+(x,y)]≥lnδd(x,y)ln(d(x,y))+|ln(ε)|-6K|ln(ε)|-2K2=|ln(ε)|lnδd(x,y)-6K+lnδd(x,y)ln(d(x,y))-2K2.The hypothesis \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x,y) < e^{-6K}\delta $$\end{document}d(x,y)<e-6Kδ gives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)} \right) - 6K > 0.$$\end{document}lnδd(x,y)-6K>0. Taking the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}ε→0 and applying the monotone convergence theorem, yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}[\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)] = \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb {E} [\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\cdot 1\!\!1_{\{\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)< \tau _{\epsilon ,-}(x,y)\}}(\omega )] = \infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[τδ,+(x,y)]=limε→0E[τδ,+(x,y)·1{τδ,+(x,y)<τϵ,-(x,y)}(ω)]=∞.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

From this lemma we know that for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_0,y_0) \in \Delta _\delta $$\end{document}(x0,y0)∈Δδ, the expected number of orbit points\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}(x_n,y_n) = \left( T^{(2)}_\omega \right) ^n (x_0,y_0) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(xn,yn)=Tω(2)n(x0,y0)before escaping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\delta $$\end{document}Δδ, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_0,y_0) \in \Delta _{ e^{-6K}\delta }$$\end{document}(x0,y0)∈Δe-6Kδ, is infinite.

The above results allow to conclude Theorem 1.1(2).

Proposition 4.3

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda =0$$\end{document}λ=0, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2\Δ,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n}\sum _{i=0}^{n-1} d\left( T^i_\omega (x),T^i_\omega (y)\right) = 0 ~\text {and}~ \limsup _{n\rightarrow \infty }d\left( T^n_\omega (x), T^n_\omega (y)\right) > 0,~\mathbb {P}-\mathrm {a.s.} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞1n∑i=0n-1dTωi(x),Tωi(y)=0andlim supn→∞dTωn(x),Tωn(y)>0,P-a.s.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2\Δ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta $$\end{document}ω∈Σϑ, consider the empirical count of iterates in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}Δε,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_\varepsilon (x,y,\omega ) = \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\frac{\#\{i\in \mathbb {N}, 0\le i \le n-1: d(T_\omega ^i(x),T_\omega ^i(y))<\varepsilon \}}{n}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Nε(x,y,ω)=limn→∞#{i∈N,0≤i≤n-1:d(Tωi(x),Tωi(y))<ε}n. We follow reasoning in [4], adapting to our setting. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_0,y_0)$$\end{document}(x0,y0) be a point outside \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}Δε and consider the orbit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_i,y_i) = \left( T_\omega ^i (x_0), T_\omega ^i (y_0)\right) $$\end{document}(xi,yi)=Tωi(x0),Tωi(y0). For almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta $$\end{document}ω∈Σϑ there is an infinite sequence of times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_i$$\end{document}Si and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_i$$\end{document}Ti, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \ge 0$$\end{document}i≥0, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 = S_0< T_0< S_1< T_1 < \cdots $$\end{document}0=S0<T0<S1<T1<⋯, such that for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j \ge 0$$\end{document}j≥0,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} T_j&= \min \left\{ i> S_j \; : \; d(x_i,y_i) < \varepsilon e^{-6K} \right\} , \\ S_{j+1}&= \min \left\{ i> T_j \; : \; d(x_i,y_i) > \varepsilon \right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Tj=mini>Sj:d(xi,yi)<εe-6K,Sj+1=mini>Tj:d(xi,yi)>ε.We can also assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x_i,y_i)$$\end{document}d(xi,yi) is never exactly \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon $$\end{document}ε, as this holds for almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta $$\end{document}ω∈Σϑ. Write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sigma _{j+1} = T_j - S_j, \qquad \tau _{j+1} = S_{j+1} - T_j. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}σj+1=Tj-Sj,τj+1=Sj+1-Tj.Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_0,y_0)$$\end{document}(x0,y0) outside \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}Δε, write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_\varepsilon (n,\omega ) = \frac{\#\{i\in \mathbb {N}, 0\le i \le n-1: d(x_i,y_i)<\varepsilon \}}{n}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Nε(n,ω)=#{i∈N,0≤i≤n-1:d(xi,yi)<ε}n.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_\sigma (n)$$\end{document}Nσ(n) be the largest index i with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_i \le n$$\end{document}Si≤n and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_\tau (n)$$\end{document}Nτ(n) be the largest index i with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_i < n$$\end{document}Ti<n. Consider, for definiteness, the case that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x_n,y_n) > \varepsilon $$\end{document}d(xn,yn)>ε. Calculate\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_\varepsilon (n,\omega )&\ge \frac{1}{T_{N_\tau (n)}} \sum _{i=1}^{T_{N_{\tau (n)}-1}} \tau _i / (\sigma _i + \tau _i) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Nε(n,ω)≥1TNτ(n)∑i=1TNτ(n)-1τi/(σi+τi)The dependence of, for instance, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma _i$$\end{document}σi on the orbit point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_{S_i}, y_{S_i})$$\end{document}(xSi,ySi), makes that we can not apply the strong law of large numbers for independent random variables. However, the arguments that lead to the strong law of large numbers, see for instance [32, Section 2.5] and [28, 29], combined with the uniform bounds on the expected escape times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma _i$$\end{document}σi do give that for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K>0$$\end{document}K>0, for almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta $$\end{document}ω∈Σϑ,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n} \sum _{i=0}^{n-1} \sigma _i&\le K. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supn→∞1n∑i=0n-1σi≤K.Likewise,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \liminf _{n\rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n} \sum _{i=0}^{n-1} \tau _i&= \infty , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim infn→∞1n∑i=0n-1τi=∞,for almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta $$\end{document}ω∈Σϑ. The case that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x_n,y_n) > \varepsilon $$\end{document}d(xn,yn)>ε is treated similarly. We get that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {P}$$\end{document}P-almost surely \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_\varepsilon (x,y,\omega )=1$$\end{document}Nε(x,y,ω)=1, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0 implying the first part of the statement.

The second part follows from Lemma 2.5. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Positive Lyapunov exponent

In this section we consider positive Lyapunov exponent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda > 0$$\end{document}λ>0. This will be assumed to hold throughout the section. Furthermore, we will denote the second zero of the moment Lyapunov function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma $$\end{document}γ.

Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0< \delta < R$$\end{document}0<δ<R and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2{\setminus } \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2\Δ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x,y) < \delta $$\end{document}d(x,y)<δ. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau (x,y,\omega )$$\end{document}τ(x,y,ω) be the minimal time with\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} d\left( \left( T^{(2)}_\omega \right) ^{\tau (x,y,\omega )} (x,y) \right) > \delta , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}dTω(2)τ(x,y,ω)(x,y)>δ,with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau (x,y,\omega )=\infty $$\end{document}τ(x,y,ω)=∞ if this does not exist.

Lemma 4.4

Let R, K be as in Lemma 4.1. Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda > 0$$\end{document}λ>0. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0< \delta < R$$\end{document}0<δ<R and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2{\setminus } \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2\Δ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x,y) < \delta $$\end{document}d(x,y)<δ we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P} \left( d(T^n_\omega (x), T^n_\omega (y)) > \delta \text { for some } n \in \mathbb {N} \right) = 1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pd(Tωn(x),Tωn(y))>δfor somen∈N=1.Moreover, for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K>0$$\end{document}K>0,4.14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\lambda } \left( \ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)} \right) - 2K \right) \le \mathbb {E}\left[ \tau (x,y,\omega ) \right] \le \frac{1}{\lambda } \left( \ln \left( \frac{\delta }{d(x,y)}\right) + 2K \right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1λlnδd(x,y)-2K≤Eτ(x,y,ω)≤1λlnδd(x,y)+2K.

Proof

Write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_n = T^n_\omega (x)$$\end{document}xn=Tωn(x) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$y_n = T^n_\omega (x)$$\end{document}yn=Tωn(x). We also use shorthand notation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau $$\end{document}τ for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau (x,y,\omega )$$\end{document}τ(x,y,ω). By Proposition 3.14 and Remark 3.15 we find, applying Doob’s stopping time theorem,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda \mathbb {E} [\tau ] \le \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi ^+ (x_\tau ,y_\tau ) - \phi ^+ (x,y) \right] \le \ln (\delta ) - \ln (d(x,y)) + 2K \end{aligned}$$\end{document}λE[τ]≤Eϕ+(xτ,yτ)-ϕ+(x,y)≤ln(δ)-ln(d(x,y))+2Kand\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda \mathbb {E} [\tau ] \ge \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi ^- (x_\tau ,y_\tau ) - \phi ^- (x,y) \right] \ge \ln (\delta ) - \ln (d(x,y)) - 2K. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}λE[τ]≥Eϕ-(xτ,yτ)-ϕ-(x,y)≥ln(δ)-ln(d(x,y))-2K.The lemma follows. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

More detailed estimates can be obtained by analysing escape times from strips \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\delta \setminus \Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}Δδ\Δε. The next lemma is the equivalent of Lemma 4.1 for positive Lyapunov exponent. Stopping times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)$$\end{document}τδ,+(x,y) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)$$\end{document}τε,-(x,y) are defined as before in (4.14.2), (4.14.2).

Lemma 4.5

Let R, K be as in Lemma 4.1. Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda > 0$$\end{document}λ>0. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \ne 0$$\end{document}γ≠0 be the negative value so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (\gamma ) = 0$$\end{document}Λ(γ)=0. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<\varepsilon<d(x,y)<\delta < R$$\end{document}0<ε<d(x,y)<δ<R, then4.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}(\min \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y), \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\}< \infty ) = 1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(min{τε,-(x,y),τδ,+(x,y)}<∞)=1.Furthermore, there exists a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa \in (0,1)$$\end{document}κ∈(0,1), such that if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<\varepsilon<d(x,y)<\kappa \delta <\kappa R$$\end{document}0<ε<d(x,y)<κδ<κR, then4.16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{K}\left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\varepsilon }\right) ^{\gamma } \le \mathbb {P}\left( \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\}\right) \le {K}\left( \frac{d(x,y)}{\varepsilon }\right) ^{\gamma }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1Kd(x,y)εγ≤P{τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)}≤Kd(x,y)εγ.

Proof

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma 4.1. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{\delta }\setminus \Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}(x,y)∈Δδ\Δε, so with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon< d(x,y) < \delta $$\end{document}ε<d(x,y)<δ. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}$$\end{document}τε,- and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _{\delta ,+}$$\end{document}τδ,+ as in (4.14.2) and (4.14.2). As before we get (4.15), that is, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\min \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-},\tau _{\delta ,+}\}<\infty $$\end{document}min{τε,-,τδ,+}<∞ for almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω.

We start with the upper bound in (4.16). Using Remark 3.17 and applying Doob’s stopping time theorem, we get4.17\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & W^-_{-\gamma }(x,y) \ge \nonumber \\ & \quad \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right) \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)},y_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)})\mid \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right] \nonumber \\ & \qquad + \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)> \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right) \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)},y_{\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)})\mid \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)> \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right] . \nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}W-γ-(x,y)≥Pτε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)EW-γ-(xτε,-(x,y),yτε,-(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)+Pτε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y)EW-γ-(xτδ,+(x,y),yτδ,+(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y).As before, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\min \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-},\delta \}$$\end{document}min{τε,-,δ} is almost surely finite (see Lemma 4.1), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)> \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right)&= 1 - \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pτε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y)=1-Pτε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y).Furthermore, for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K>1$$\end{document}K>1 as in Proposition 3.16, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)},y_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)})\mid \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right]&\ge \frac{\varepsilon ^{\gamma }}{K}, \\ \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)},y_{\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)})\mid \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)> \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right]&\ge \frac{\delta ^{\gamma }}{K}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}EW-γ-(xτε,-(x,y),yτε,-(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)≥εγK,EW-γ-(xτδ,+(x,y),yτδ,+(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y)≥δγK,and also \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K d(x,y)^{\gamma } \ge W^-_{-\gamma }(x,y)$$\end{document}Kd(x,y)γ≥W-γ-(x,y). Then (4.17) yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y) < \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right)&\le \left( {K^2} d(x,y)^{\gamma } - \delta ^{\gamma } \right) / \left( \varepsilon ^{\gamma } - \delta ^{\gamma } \right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pτε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)≤K2d(x,y)γ-δγ/εγ-δγ.From this and a condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x,y) \le \kappa \delta $$\end{document}d(x,y)≤κδ for small enough \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa $$\end{document}κ, we get the stated bound. The lower bound in (4.16) is derived similarly. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Using the above lemma we get the following statement in which we discuss, for orbit pieces starting in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _{\kappa \delta }\setminus \Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}Δκδ\Δε until escape from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\delta $$\end{document}Δδ, the expected number of points in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}Δε. It is similar to Lemma 5.3, but for positive Lyapunov exponent.

The results in this section easily allow to conclude Theorem 1.1(3).

Proposition 4.6

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda > 0$$\end{document}λ>0, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2\Δ,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\frac{1}{n}\sum _{i=0}^{n-1} d \left( T^i_\omega (x), T^i_\omega (y)\right) > 0 ~\text {and}~ \liminf _{n\rightarrow \infty }d\left( T^n_\omega (x),T^n_\omega (y)\right) = 0,~\mathbb {P}-\mathrm {a.s.} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞1n∑i=0n-1dTωi(x),Tωi(y)>0andlim infn→∞dTωn(x),Tωn(y)=0,P-a.s.

Proof

The reasoning follows Proposition 4.3. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \mathbb {T}^2\setminus \Delta $$\end{document}(x,y)∈T2\Δ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta $$\end{document}ω∈Σϑ, consider the empirical count of iterates in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}Δε,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_\varepsilon (x,y,\omega ) = \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\frac{\#\{i\in \mathbb {N}, 0\le i \le n-1: d(T_\omega ^i(x),T_\omega ^i(y))<\varepsilon \}}{n}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Nε(x,y,ω)=limn→∞#{i∈N,0≤i≤n-1:d(Tωi(x),Tωi(y))<ε}n.By Lemmas 4.4 and 2.3 and the strong law of large numbers, we get that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {P}$$\end{document}P-almost surely \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_\varepsilon (x,y,\omega )<1 $$\end{document}Nε(x,y,ω)<1, for a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0 implying the first part of the statement. The second part follows from Lemma 2.5. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Negative Lyapunov exponent

In this section we consider negative Lyapunov exponent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda <0$$\end{document}λ<0. This will be assumed to hold throughout the section. The following lemma can be obtained by the construction of local stable manifolds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^n_\omega $$\end{document}Tωn of points in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ, which exists for almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Sigma _\vartheta $$\end{document}ω∈Σϑ [49]. We provide a proof along the lines of our reasoning in previous sections, compare also [10].

Lemma 4.7

Let R, K be as in Lemma 4.1. Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda < 0$$\end{document}λ<0. There is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0< \chi <1$$\end{document}0<χ<1 so that the following holds. For each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0< \delta < R$$\end{document}0<δ<R there is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0< \delta ' < \delta $$\end{document}0<δ′<δ, so that for (x, y) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d(x,y) < \delta '$$\end{document}d(x,y)<δ′,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P} \left( d( T^n_\omega (x) , T^n_\omega (y)) < \delta \text { for all } n \text { and } \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } d( T^n_\omega (x) , T^n_\omega (y)) = 0 \right)&> \chi . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pd(Tωn(x),Tωn(y))<δfor allnandlimn→∞d(Tωn(x),Tωn(y))=0>χ.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma >0$$\end{document}γ>0 given by Lemma 3.4 be so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda (\gamma )=0$$\end{document}Λ(γ)=0. The first part of the proof is similar to the proof of Lemmas 4.1 and 4.5. Take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y) \in \Delta _{\delta }{\setminus } \Delta _\varepsilon $$\end{document}(x,y)∈Δδ\Δε, so with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \le d(x,y) < \delta $$\end{document}ε≤d(x,y)<δ. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)$$\end{document}τε,-(x,y) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)$$\end{document}τδ,+(x,y) as in (4.14.2) and (4.14.2). As before we get (4.15), that is, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\min \{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y),\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\}<\infty $$\end{document}min{τε,-(x,y),τδ,+(x,y)}<∞ for almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω. Thus, as in Lemma 4.1,4.18\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)> \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right)&= 1 - \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pτε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y)=1-Pτε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y).Using Remark 3.17 and applying Doob’s stopping time theorem, we get4.19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & W^-_{-\gamma }(x,y) \ge \nonumber \\ & \quad \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right) \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)},y_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)})\mid \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right] \nonumber \\ & \qquad + \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)> \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right) \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)},y_{\tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)})\mid \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)> \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right] . \nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}W-γ-(x,y)≥Pτε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)EW-γ-(xτε,-(x,y),yτε,-(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)+Pτε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y)EW-γ-(xτδ,+(x,y),yτδ,+(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y).For some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K>1$$\end{document}K>1 as in Proposition 3.16, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)},y_{\tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)})\mid \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right]&\ge \frac{\varepsilon ^\gamma }{K}, \\ \mathbb {E} \left[ W^-_{-\gamma }(x_{\tau \end{aligned}$$\end{document}EW-γ-(xτε,-(x,y),yτε,-(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)≥εγK,EW-γ-(xτδ,+(x,y),yτδ,+(x,y))∣τε,-(x,y)>τδ,+(x,y)≥δγK,and also \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${K} d(x,y)^{\gamma } \ge W^-_{-\gamma }(x,y)$$\end{document}Kd(x,y)γ≥W-γ-(x,y). Using (4.18), (4.19) yields4.20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb {P} \left( \tau _{\varepsilon ,-}(x,y)< \tau _{\delta ,+}(x,y)\right)&\ge \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \left( {K^2} d(x,y)^\gamma - \delta ^\gamma \right) / \left( \varepsilon ^\gamma - \delta ^\gamma \right) \nonumber \\&= 1 - {K^2} \left( \frac{d(x,y) }{ \delta } \right) ^\gamma . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limε→0Pτε,-(x,y)<τδ,+(x,y)≥limε→0K2d(x,y)γ-δγ/εγ-δγ=1-K2d(x,y)δγ.The limit exists because the probability is monotone decreasing as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}ε→0.
